<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We construct smooth forces of fixed compact spatial support for three-dimensional incompressible Navier–Stokes flow from rest whose particle at the origin detects halting by entering a fixed half-space. Every mixed derivative of the force and velocity decays at rate $`O((1+t)^{-1-j})`$ for time order <i>j</i>. Three scalar potentials lift a coded rectangle, perform its instruction, and lower its image; an unbounded logarithmic clock supplies the decay. We also realize area-changing prefix instructions on an invariant torus plane, and construct a planar Hamiltonian processor admitting periodic forcing, stationary forcing after startup, and a velocity-field detector through a companion diffusion theorem.

- **C1** (construction, [source](#stmt-bcs-scalar-theorem)): Fix a positive computable viscosity $\nu$. A deterministic one-tape machine and finite input effectively determine a smooth force on $[0,\infty)\times\mathbb R^3$, supported in one compact spatial set for all time, whose solution from zero velocity has pressure zero and satisfies $$\\|\partial_t^jD_x^\alpha u(t)\\|_\infty+ \\|\partial_t^jD_x^\alpha f(t)\\|_\infty \le C_{j,\alpha}(1+t)^{-1-j}$$ for every $j\ge0$ and spatial multi-index $\alpha$. The particle initially at the origin enters $\{x_1<-1\}$ exactly when the machine halts. Every displayed derivative belongs to space-time $L^2$, and the kinetic energy is uniformly bounded. Uniqueness holds among smooth classical solutions with, for every finite $T$, $$u\in C([0,T];H^2)\cap C^1([0,T];L^2),\quad p\in C([0,T];H^1),\quad \sup_{[0,T]\times\mathbb R^3}(\|u\|+\|\nabla u\|)<\infty.$$ The formulas remain valid for any fixed positive real viscosity, with evaluation relative to that parameter.
  - Explanation: An effective compact spatial force realizes machine halting as a fixed material event, with derivative decay and uniqueness in the stated whole-space comparison class.
- **C2** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-b-comparison)): Fix $\nu>0$ and let $U$ be a prescribed smooth divergence-free velocity with $U(0)=0$, bounded together with its spatial gradient on every finite time interval. In the whole-space case assume also the velocity conditions in <a href="#eq:comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:comparison">[eq:comparison]</a>. Then the force $$f=\mathcal F_\nu[U]:=U_t+(U\cdot\nabla)U-\nu\Delta U$$ has $(U,0)$ as its unique solution in the stated comparison class. Its material trajectories exist uniquely for all finite forward times and give smooth diffeomorphisms at each such time. A mean-zero velocity on the torus has a mean-zero residual force.
  - Explanation: A prescribed divergence-free velocity determines its residual force and is the unique solution in the stated comparison class; this does not assert existence for arbitrary large data.
- **C3** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-bcs-clock)): Fix $\nu>0$. Let $W(s,x)$ be a smooth divergence-free field for $s\ge0$, on a flat torus or on $\mathbb R^3$. Suppose all its mixed derivatives are bounded, $W(0)=0$, and in the whole-space case its spatial support lies in a fixed compact set. Let $g:[0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)$ be smooth, nondecreasing, onto, with $g(0)=0$. Then $$\begin{aligned} u(t,x)&=g'(t)W(g(t),x),\\ f(t,x)&=g''(t)W(g(t),x)-\nu g'(t)\Delta W(g(t),x)\notag\\ &\quad +(g'(t))^2[\partial_sW+(W\cdot\nabla)W](g(t),x) \label{bcs:clock-force} \end{aligned}$$ gives a prescribed solution with pressure zero and zero initial velocity. Its material flow is $X_u(t,a)=X_W(g(t),a)$, and hence preserves every all-time reachability event. If $(W\cdot\nabla)W=0$ identically, $f$ is divergence free. Spatial mean zero, when imposed on $W$, is inherited by $u$ and $f$.* *For $g(t)=\log(1+t)$, every $j\ge0$ and spatial multi-index $\alpha$ satisfy $$\label{bcs:log-bounds} \\|\partial_t^jD_x^\alpha u(t)\\|_\infty+ \\|\partial_t^jD_x^\alpha f(t)\\|_\infty \le C_{j,\alpha}(1+t)^{-1-j}.$$ All these derivatives belong to $L^2([0,\infty);L^\infty)$; on a torus or with fixed compact support they also belong to space-time $L^2$.
  - Explanation: An onto clock preserves all-time material reachability. The logarithmic choice yields the stated derivative decay and integrability.
- **C4** (construction, [source](#stmt-bcs-delayed-recorder)): At successive ready states the auxiliary table has exactly the ordinary work tape, head and state. It reaches an $R_{h,d}$ exactly when the ordinary machine halts. Every ordinary step uses a positive finite number of auxiliary steps. On the full domain of the auxiliary table, a destination state fixes the incoming displacement, and that state together with the full written symbol determines the incoming rule.
  - Explanation: The delayed-head recorder retains erased information, restores ordinary configurations at ready states, and makes incoming rules recognizable on the full allowed domain.
- **C5** (bound, [source](#stmt-bcs-power-clock)): Under the hypotheses of Lemma <a href="#bcs:clock" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bcs:clock">3</a>, set $g(t)=(1+t)^q-1$, where $q=1/3$ or $q=1/4$, and put $\beta=1-q$. All mixed derivatives of the velocity and force in <a href="#bcs:clock-force" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bcs:clock-force">[bcs:clock-force]</a> are bounded, and for every fixed $j,\alpha$, $$\\|\partial_t^jD_x^\alpha u(t)\\|_\infty+ \\|\partial_t^jD_x^\alpha f(t)\\|_\infty \le C_{j,\alpha}(1+t)^{-\beta}.$$ In particular each is square-integrable in time in spatial supremum norm. The cube-root exponent is $2/3$ and the fourth-root exponent is $3/4$.
  - Explanation: The root clocks give decay with a common exponent at each fixed mixed derivative order; their constants need not be uniform across derivative orders.
- **C6** (construction, [source](#stmt-bcs-autonomous-profile)): Let $Z(x)$ be a smooth divergence-free field on a flat torus. Let $\sigma$ be the smooth step which is zero for $t\le0$, one for $t\ge1$, and satisfies $\sigma(t)+\sigma(1-t)=1$. Define $$\alpha_0(t)=\sigma(2t-1),\qquad \alpha_1(t)=\frac{\sigma(2t-1)}{1+t},\qquad u(t,x)=\alpha(t)Z(x).$$ For either choice $\alpha=\alpha_0,\alpha_1$, the residual force is $$\label{bcs:autonomous-force} f=\alpha'Z+\alpha^2(Z\cdot\nabla)Z-\nu\alpha\Delta Z.$$ It has bounded mixed derivatives, gives zero initial velocity and pressure zero, and follows the spatial flow of $Z$ at the onto time $s(t)=\int_0^t\alpha$. For $\alpha_0$, $s(t)=t-3/4$ for $t\ge1$, and the force is stationary there. For $\alpha_1$, $$s(t)=s(1)+\log\frac{1+t}{2}\quad(t\ge1),$$ and the force tends uniformly to zero and belongs to space-time $L^2$. If $\int Z=0$, both forces have zero mean. In general their means equal $\alpha'\int Z$.
  - Explanation: Startup profiles applied to an autonomous spatial field produce stationary or decaying forcing while keeping accumulated spatial time onto. The mean force is determined by the startup derivative.
- **C7** (construction, [source](#stmt-bcs-prefix-theorem)): For fixed computable $\nu>0$, every deterministic one-tape machine and finite input effectively determine a smooth mean-zero force on the unit torus, with zero initial velocity, such that $$\\|\partial_t^jD_x^\alpha f(t)\\|_\infty\le C_{j,\alpha}(1+t)^{-1-j}$$ for every $j,\alpha$. The unique global smooth velocity satisfies the same bounds. Its particle from $(1/4,1/2,1/2)$ enters $\{1/2<x_1<1\}$ exactly when the machine halts. One choice of force has pressure zero; projecting that force gives a divergence-free choice with the same velocity and a changed normalized pressure. For a fixed machine, the repeated motion before the clock change is independent of the input; only its initial loading motion changes.
  - Explanation: Prefix instructions are realized on an invariant torus plane with transverse compensation for area change, and the fixed particle detects halting with logarithmic derivative decay.
- **C8** (construction, [source](#stmt-bcs-prefix-symbolic)): The full domain cylinders of these rules are pairwise disjoint, and their full image cylinders are pairwise disjoint. Initialized checkpoints recover the ordinary machine configurations. Each ordinary transition takes a positive finite number of prefix steps, and a terminal checkpoint occurs exactly on halting.
  - Explanation: The prefix recorder gives disjoint full source and image cylinders, finite scans at initialized checkpoints, and the ordinary halting equivalence.
- **C9** (construction, [source](#stmt-bcs-prefix-planar)): Let $D_i,E_i$, $1\le i\le m$, be rational axis-aligned closed rectangles of positive side lengths. Suppose the $D_i$ are pairwise disjoint in $\Omega_-=(0,1/2)\times(0,1)$, the $E_i$ are pairwise disjoint in $\Omega=(0,1)^2$, and each $E_i$ lies entirely in one open half of $\Omega$. There is an effective smooth field $v_*(s,Y)$ supported in $(0,1)\times\Omega$ whose time-one map on each full $D_i$ is its positive diagonal affine identification with $E_i$. If $E_i\subset\Omega_-$, the whole trajectory from $D_i$ remains in $\Omega_-$. No incompressibility is asserted in this planar lemma.
  - Explanation: A finite geometric interpolation realizes positive diagonal rectangle maps and keeps a trajectory in the left half whenever its target is there. Planar incompressibility is not claimed.
- **C10** (construction, [source](#stmt-bcs-expanded-compiler)): The initialized table reaches a stop state exactly when the ordinary partial-table machine halts. Otherwise it has defined transitions forever. On its full allowed domain, the destination state and full written symbol determine a unique incoming rule; the destination alone determines its displacement.
  - Explanation: The expanded partial-table recorder reproduces halting and has a uniquely recognizable incoming rule on its full allowed domain.
- **C11** (construction, [source](#stmt-bcs-expanded-processor)): There is an effective smooth planar Hamiltonian field $V(s,Y)$, period one in $s$ and zero near each integer phase, with support in $(1/8,7/8)^2$ and spatial mean zero, whose period map equals $T_i$ on each full $R_i$. All mixed derivatives have effective uniform bounds. A rectangle with a non-stop destination stays in $1/8<Y_1<3/8$ throughout its transition. The trajectory from $c_L$ enters $2/3<Y_1<5/6$ exactly when the machine halts, and a halting visit occurs at an integer phase.
  - Explanation: A periodic planar Hamiltonian processor realizes full rectangle maps while controlling intermediate paths and detecting halting at an integer phase.
- **C12** (construction, [source](#stmt-bcs-expanded-material)): For each fixed computable $\nu>0$, the machine and input effectively determine a smooth period-one mean-zero force on $\mathbb T^3$, all of whose derivatives are bounded, such that the solution from zero velocity has its particle from $X_*$ enter $\mathcal S$ exactly when the machine halts. A separate choice of force is stationary for $t\ge1$, also with bounded derivatives and the same fixed event. Both constructions have pressure zero. The stationary choice has a generally nonzero mean force during startup.
  - Explanation: Separate periodic and eventually stationary force prescriptions realize the same fixed particle event; the stationary prescription generally has a nonzero startup mean.
- **C13** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-bcs-expanded-eulerian)): For fixed computable $\nu>0$, the machine and input effectively determine a smooth prescribed force on $[0,\infty)\times\mathbb T^3$, whose unique global smooth solution from zero velocity and with pressure zero satisfies $$\exists t\ge0\ \exists X\in\mathcal S:\ u_3(t,X)>1/2 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\text{the machine halts}.$$ Uniqueness holds in the torus classical comparison class of Section <a href="#sec:analytic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:analytic">2</a>: the velocity, its time derivative and spatial derivatives through order two are continuous on each finite closed cylinder, and the periodic pressure and its gradient are continuous, with mean-zero pressure normalization. No uniform large-time amplitude bound or mean-zero condition is asserted for this force.
  - Explanation: The companion diffusion detector converts the same processor into a velocity threshold event, without asserting a uniform large-time force bound or zero force mean.
- **C14** (theorem, [source](#stmt-auto-corollary-1)): There is no algorithm deciding the fixed material reachability event from every force description produced by Theorem <a href="#bcs:scalar-theorem" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bcs:scalar-theorem">1</a>. The same conclusion holds separately for the force descriptions and fixed observations of Theorem <a href="#bcs:prefix-theorem" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bcs:prefix-theorem">7</a>, Proposition <a href="#bcs:expanded-material" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bcs:expanded-material">12</a>, and Corollary <a href="#bcs:expanded-eulerian" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bcs:expanded-eulerian">13</a>.
  - Explanation: The effective halting equivalences obstruct a decision algorithm for each specified family of force descriptions and its fixed observation.

A deterministic one-tape machine and finite input effectively determine a smooth force on $[0,\\infty)\\times\\mathbb R^3$, supported in one compact spatial set for all time, whose solution from zero velocity has pressure zero and satisfies $$\\|\\partial_t^jD_x^\\alpha u(t)\\|_\\infty+\n \\|\\partial_t^jD_x^\\alpha f(t)\\|_\\infty\n \\le C_{j,\\alpha}(1+t)^{-1-j}$$ for every $j\\ge0$ and spatial multi-index $\\alpha$. The particle initially at the origin enters $\\{x_1<-1\\}$ exactly when the machine halts. Every displayed derivative belongs to space-time $L^2$, and the kinetic energy is uniformly bounded. Uniqueness holds among smooth classical solutions with, for every finite $T$, $$u\\in C([0,T];H^2)\\cap C^1([0,T];L^2),\\quad\n p\\in C([0,T];H^1),\\quad\n \\sup_{[0,T]\\times\\mathbb R^3}(|u|+|\\nabla u|)<\\infty.$$ The formulas remain valid for any fixed positive real viscosity, with evaluation relative to that parameter.",
        "source_id": "PM-376.8"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

          "exact_quote": "Fix a positive computable viscosity $\\nu$. The particle initially at the origin enters $\\{x_1<-1\\}$ exactly when the machine halts. Every displayed derivative belongs to space-time $L^2$, and the kinetic energy is uniformly bounded. Uniqueness holds among smooth classical solutions with, for every finite $T$, $$u\\in C([0,T];H^2)\\cap C^1([0,T];L^2),\\quad\n p\\in C([0,T];H^1),\\quad\n \\sup_{[0,T]\\times\\mathbb R^3}(|u|+|\\nabla u|)<\\infty.$$ The formulas remain valid for any fixed positive real viscosity, with evaluation relative to that parameter.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-b-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $\\nu>0$ and let $U$ be a prescribed smooth divergence-free velocity with $U(0)=0$, bounded together with its spatial gradient on every finite time interval. In the whole-space case assume also the velocity conditions in <a href=\"#eq:comparison\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:comparison\">[eq:comparison]</a>. Then the force $$f=\\mathcal F_\\nu[U]:=U_t+(U\\cdot\\nabla)U-\\nu\\Delta U$$ has $(U,0)$ as its unique solution in the stated comparison class. Its material trajectories exist uniquely for all finite forward times and give smooth diffeomorphisms at each such time. A mean-zero velocity on the torus has a mean-zero residual force.",
        "source_id": "PM-376.8"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-bcs-clock",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $\\nu>0$. Let $W(s,x)$ be a smooth divergence-free field for $s\\ge0$, on a flat torus or on $\\mathbb R^3$. Suppose all its mixed derivatives are bounded, $W(0)=0$, and in the whole-space case its spatial support lies in a fixed compact set. Let $g:[0,\\infty)\\to[0,\\infty)$ be smooth, nondecreasing, onto, with $g(0)=0$. Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n u(t,x)&=g'(t)W(g(t),x),\\\\\n f(t,x)&=g''(t)W(g(t),x)-\\nu g'(t)\\Delta W(g(t),x)\\notag\\\\\n &\\quad +(g'(t))^2[\\partial_sW+(W\\cdot\\nabla)W](g(t),x)\n \\label{bcs:clock-force}\n\\end{aligned}$$ gives a prescribed solution with pressure zero and zero initial velocity. Its material flow is $X_u(t,a)=X_W(g(t),a)$, and hence preserves every all-time reachability event. If $(W\\cdot\\nabla)W=0$ identically, $f$ is divergence free. Spatial mean zero, when imposed on $W$, is inherited by $u$ and $f$.*\n\n\n*For $g(t)=\\log(1+t)$, every $j\\ge0$ and spatial multi-index $\\alpha$ satisfy $$\\label{bcs:log-bounds}\n \\|\\partial_t^jD_x^\\alpha u(t)\\|_\\infty+\n \\|\\partial_t^jD_x^\\alpha f(t)\\|_\\infty\n \\le C_{j,\\alpha}(1+t)^{-1-j}.$$ All these derivatives belong to $L^2([0,\\infty);L^\\infty)$; on a torus or with fixed compact support they also belong to space-time $L^2$.",
        "source_id": "PM-376.8"
      },
      "evidence": [

Its material flow is $X_u(t,a)=X_W(g(t),a)$, and hence preserves every all-time reachability event. If $(W\\cdot\\nabla)W=0$ identically, $f$ is divergence free.         "exact_quote": "At successive ready states the auxiliary table has exactly the ordinary work tape, head and state. It reaches an $R_{h,d}$ exactly when the ordinary machine halts.         "anchor": "stmt-bcs-autonomous-profile",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Z(x)$ be a smooth divergence-free field on a flat torus. Let $\\sigma$ be the smooth step which is zero for $t\\le0$, one for $t\\ge1$, and satisfies $\\sigma(t)+\\sigma(1-t)=1$. Define $$\\alpha_0(t)=\\sigma(2t-1),\\qquad\n \\alpha_1(t)=\\frac{\\sigma(2t-1)}{1+t},\\qquad\n u(t,x)=\\alpha(t)Z(x).$$ For either choice $\\alpha=\\alpha_0,\\alpha_1$, the residual force is $$\\label{bcs:autonomous-force}\n f=\\alpha'Z+\\alpha^2(Z\\cdot\\nabla)Z-\\nu\\alpha\\Delta Z.$$ It has bounded mixed derivatives, gives zero initial velocity and pressure zero, and follows the spatial flow of $Z$ at the onto time $s(t)=\\int_0^t\\alpha$. For $\\alpha_0$, $s(t)=t-3/4$ for $t\\ge1$, and the force is stationary there. For $\\alpha_1$, $$s(t)=s(1)+\\log\\frac{1+t}{2}\\quad(t\\ge1),$$ and the force tends uniformly to zero and belongs to space-time $L^2$. If $\\int Z=0$, both forces have zero mean. In general their means equal $\\alpha'\\int Z$.",
        "source_id": "PM-376.8"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-bcs-autonomous-profile",
          "exact_quote": "Let $Z(x)$ be a smooth divergence-free field on a flat torus. For $\\alpha_0$, $s(t)=t-3/4$ for $t\\ge1$, and the force is stationary there.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-bcs-prefix-theorem",
        "exact_quote": "For fixed computable $\\nu>0$, every deterministic one-tape machine and finite input effectively determine a smooth mean-zero force on the unit torus, with zero initial velocity, such that $$\\|\\partial_t^jD_x^\\alpha f(t)\\|_\\infty\\le C_{j,\\alpha}(1+t)^{-1-j}$$ for every $j,\\alpha$. The unique global smooth velocity satisfies the same bounds. Its particle from $(1/4,1/2,1/2)$ enters $\\{1/2<x_1<1\\}$ exactly when the machine halts. One choice of force has pressure zero; projecting that force gives a divergence-free choice with the same velocity and a changed normalized pressure. For a fixed machine, the repeated motion before the clock change is independent of the input; only its initial loading motion changes.",
        "source_id": "PM-376.8"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-bcs-prefix-theorem",

One choice of force has pressure zero; projecting that force gives a divergence-free choice with the same velocity and a changed normalized pressure.           "source_id": "PM-376.8"

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **residual force** ([source](#stmt-lem-b-comparison)): Then the force $$f=\mathcal F_\nu[U]:=U_t+(U\cdot\nabla)U-\nu\Delta U$$ has $(U,0)$ as its unique solution in the stated comparison class.
- **onto processor clock** ([source](#stmt-bcs-clock)): Let $g:[0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)$ be smooth, nondecreasing, onto, with $g(0)=0$.
- **material flow** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For a material label $a$, write $X_u(t,a)$ for the solution of $\partial_tX_u(t,a)=u(t,X_u(t,a))$, $X_u(0,a)=a$.
- **material detector** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A material detector asks whether this one particle ever enters a fixed open set $O$.
- **smooth step** ([source](#bcs:smooth-step)): We use the explicit smooth step $$\label{bcs:smooth-step} \rho(v)=\begin{cases}e^{-1/v},&v>0,\\0,&v\le0,\end{cases} \qquad \sigma(v)=\frac{\rho(v)}{\rho(v)+\rho(1-v)}.$$
- **tape coding and first-letter interval** ([source](#pm-h-9)): Put $$E(a_1a_2\cdots)=\sum_{n\ge1}c(a_n)G^{-n},\qquad I_a=[c(a)/G,(c(a)+1)/G].$$
- **scalar-potential differential operators** ([source](#pm-h-10)): Define the two divergence-free differential operators $$D_yP=(-\partial_zP,0,\partial_xP),\qquad D_zP=(\partial_yP,-\partial_xP,0).$$
- **prefix coding** ([source](#pm-h-15)): Set $$\gamma(w)=\sum_{j\ge1}D(w_j)B^{-j},\quad c(\alpha)=\sum_{j=1}^{\|\alpha\|}D(\alpha_j)B^{-j},\quad I(\alpha)=[c(\alpha),c(\alpha)+B^{-\|\alpha\|}].$$
- **transverse divergence lift** ([source](#bcs:prefix-lift)): Define $$\label{bcs:prefix-lift} W(s,Y,z)=\bigl(\eta'(z)v(s,Y),-\eta(z)\operatorname{div}_Yv(s,Y)\bigr).$$
- **fixed cluster bases, particle and observation strip** ([source](#bcs:expanded-section)): Fix $$c_L=(1/4,1/4),\quad c_R=(3/4,1/4),\quad X_*=(1/4,1/4,1/4),\quad \mathcal S=\{X\in\mathbb T^3:2/3<X_1<5/6\}.$$

      "exact_definition": "Define $$\\label{bcs:prefix-lift}\n W(s,Y,z)=\\bigl(\\eta'(z)v(s,Y),-\\eta(z)\\operatorname{div}_Yv(s,Y)\\bigr).$$",

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **S1** [claim](#stmt-lem-b-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-b-comparison): Difference-energy comparison -> Unique prescribed solution and finite-time material diffeomorphisms
- **S2** [claim](#stmt-bcs-clock) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bcs-clock): Chain rule and onto time change -> Preserved all-time reachability with logarithmic decay
- **S3** [claim](#stmt-bcs-delayed-recorder) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bcs-delayed-recorder): Delayed-head history recording -> Recognizable incoming rules and finite recorder scans
- **S4** [claim](#stmt-bcs-scalar-theorem) / [proof](#bcs:scalar-section): Separated rectangle lift, reciprocal interpolation, descent and loading -> Compact spatial force and exact fixed material detector
- **S5** [claim](#stmt-bcs-power-clock) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bcs-power-clock): Repeated differentiation of root-clock coefficients -> Square-integrable derivative decay at fixed orders
- **S6** [claim](#stmt-bcs-autonomous-profile) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bcs-autonomous-profile): Autonomous residual identity and startup symmetry -> Onto spatial time and stationary or decaying force
- **S7** [claim](#stmt-bcs-prefix-symbolic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bcs-prefix-symbolic): Prefix-history cylinder disjointness and finite scans -> Ordinary configurations at checkpoints and injective full rules
- **S8** [claim](#stmt-bcs-prefix-planar) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bcs-prefix-planar): Shrink, move and expand with rational routes -> Positive diagonal affine maps with complete path control
- **S9** [claim](#stmt-bcs-prefix-theorem) / [proof](#bcs:prefix-section): Transverse divergence compensation and logarithmic slowdown -> Mean-zero torus force and exact fixed particle event
- **S10** [claim](#stmt-bcs-expanded-compiler) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bcs-expanded-compiler): Partial-table history recording and checkpoint induction -> Recognizable predecessor rules and exact stopping equivalence
- **S11** [claim](#stmt-bcs-expanded-processor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bcs-expanded-processor): Hamiltonian routing with disjoint moving collars -> Periodic planar processor with controlled intermediate trajectories
- **S12** [claim](#stmt-bcs-expanded-material) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bcs-expanded-material): Periodic planar lift and autonomous spatial suspension -> Periodic and eventually stationary material detectors
- **S13** [claim](#stmt-bcs-expanded-eulerian) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bcs-expanded-eulerian): Companion injection, stirring and waiting theorem with viscosity scaling -> A third-component velocity threshold equivalent to halting
- **S14** [claim](#stmt-auto-corollary-1) / [proof](#proof-stmt-auto-corollary-1): Reduction from halting by effective composition -> No decision algorithm for the stated observation families

<!-- PM:BEGIN pm.roadmap.v2 -->

{
  "generation_status": "COMPLETE",
  "nonconstructive_steps": [
    {
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-auto-corollary-1",
          "exact_quote": "There is no algorithm deciding the fixed material reachability event from every force description produced by Theorem <a href=\"#bcs:scalar-theorem\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"bcs:scalar-theorem\">1</a>. The same conclusion holds separately for the force descriptions and fixed observations of Theorem <a href=\"#bcs:prefix-theorem\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"bcs:prefix-theorem\">7</a>, Proposition <a href=\"#bcs:expanded-material\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"bcs:expanded-material\">12</a>, and Corollary <a href=\"#bcs:expanded-eulerian\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"bcs:expanded-eulerian\">13</a>.",
          "source_id": "PM-376.8"
        }
      ],
      "reason": "The decision obstruction is a contradiction from halting undecidability, rather than an algorithm deciding the event.",
      "step_id": "S14"
    }
  ],
  "pm_id": "PM-376.8",
  "schema": "pm.roadmap.v2",
  "steps": [
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-b-comparison",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-b-comparison",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $\\nu>0$ and let $U$ be a prescribed smooth divergence-free velocity with $U(0)=0$, bounded together with its spatial gradient on every finite time interval. In the whole-space case assume also the velocity conditions in <a href=\"#eq:comparison\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:comparison\">[eq:comparison]</a>. Then the force $$f=\\mathcal F_\\nu[U]:=U_t+(U\\cdot\\nabla)U-\\nu\\Delta U$$ has $(U,0)$ as its unique solution in the stated comparison class. Its material trajectories exist uniquely for all finite forward times and give smooth diffeomorphisms at each such time. A mean-zero velocity on the torus has a mean-zero residual force.",
          "source_id": "PM-376.8"
        }
      ],
      "id": "S1",
      "inputs": [
        "Prescribed divergence-free velocity and stated comparison class"
      ],
      "method": "Difference-energy comparison",
      "output": "Unique prescribed solution and finite-time material diffeomorphisms",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-b-comparison"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-bcs-clock",
      "depends_on": [
        "S1"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-bcs-clock",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $\\nu>0$. Let $W(s,x)$ be a smooth divergence-free field for $s\\ge0$, on a flat torus or on $\\mathbb R^3$. Suppose all its mixed derivatives are bounded, $W(0)=0$, and in the whole-space case its spatial support lies in a fixed compact set. Let $g:[0,\\infty)\\to[0,\\infty)$ be smooth, nondecreasing, onto, with $g(0)=0$. Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n u(t,x)&=g'(t)W(g(t),x),\\\\\n f(t,x)&=g''(t)W(g(t),x)-\\nu g'(t)\\Delta W(g(t),x)\\notag\\\\\n &\\quad +(g'(t))^2[\\partial_sW+(W\\cdot\\nabla)W](g(t),x)\n \\label{bcs:clock-force}\n\\end{aligned}$$ gives a prescribed solution with pressure zero and zero initial velocity. Its material flow is $X_u(t,a)=X_W(g(t),a)$, and hence preserves every all-time reachability event. If $(W\\cdot\\nabla)W=0$ identically, $f$ is divergence free. Spatial mean zero, when imposed on $W$, is inherited by $u$ and $f$.*\n\n\n*For $g(t)=\\log(1+t)$, every $j\\ge0$ and spatial multi-index $\\alpha$ satisfy $$\\label{bcs:log-bounds}\n \\|\\partial_t^jD_x^\\alpha u(t)\\|_\\infty+\n \\|\\partial_t^jD_x^\\alpha f(t)\\|_\\infty\n \\le C_{j,\\alpha}(1+t)^{-1-j}.$$ All these derivatives belong to $L^2([0,\\infty);L^\\infty)$; on a torus or with fixed compact support they also belong to space-time $L^2$.",
          "source_id": "PM-376.8"
        }

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **TAPE_MAP**: The normalized affine instructions implement the tape update and preserve planar area with positive diagonal scaling.
- **FULL_PATH**: Every transition with a non-stop destination remains in the left observation column throughout the continuous motion.
- **ONTO_CLOCK**: An onto clock preserves all-time material reachability.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **FINITE_HORIZON**: Infer nonhalting from a finite simulation without a detector visit. -> Later finite logical times remain unobserved.
- **ROBUSTNESS**: Treat rounded coordinates as a perturbation-stable halting detector. -> The exact construction supplies no uniform perturbation tolerance.
- **OVERWRITE**: Directly embed an information-erasing instruction in an invertible smooth motion. -> Distinct predecessors cannot be recovered from an overwritten symbol.
- **BOUNDED_CLOCK**: Replace the onto clock by a speed with finite accumulated time. -> The flow may stop before later instructions execute.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The normalized rectangle instructions are finite explicit affine maps. The code checks their local tape action; the smooth flow realization and all-time halting equivalence remain outside this finite test.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON list of cases [m, left_digits, right_digits, written_digit, displacement].
# PRECONDITIONS: 1 <= m <= 3; digit lists have length 1 or 2 and odd digits in [1, 2*m-1]; displacement in {-1,0,1}; infinite tails repeat digit 1.
# OUTPUTS: JSON pairs of exact rational coordinate strings for the updated tape.
# TIME: O(cases * digit-list length) rational arithmetic operations; cases <= 2000.
# SPACE: O(cases * digit-list length) stored exact rational data.
# ARITHMETIC: python-flint fmpq; no floating point or solver witnesses.
# FAILURE: Invalid input raises an error; no result outside the finite input contract.
import sys, json
from flint import fmpq
cases = json.loads(sys.stdin.read())
assert isinstance(cases, list) and 0 < len(cases) <= 2000
def coordinate(digits, base):
    total = fmpq(1, base-1)
    for digit in reversed(digits):
        total = (digit + total) / base
    return total
def text(q):
    return str(q.numerator) + '/' + str(q.denominator)
results = []
for m, left, right, written, move in cases:
    assert type(m) is int and 1 <= m <= 3
    base = 2*m+1
    assert type(move) is int and move in (-1,0,1)
    assert all(isinstance(ds,list) and 1 <= len(ds) <= 2 for ds in (left,right))
    assert all(type(v) is int and v in range(1,2*m,2) for v in left+right+[written])
    x = coordinate(left, base)
    y = coordinate(right, base)
    correction = fmpq(written-right[0], base)
    if move == 1:
        nx = (written+x)/base
        ny = base*y-right[0]
    elif move == -1:
        nx = base*x-left[0]
        ny = (left[0]+y+correction)/base
    else:
        nx = x
        ny = y+correction
    results.append([text(nx),text(ny)])
print(json.dumps(results,separators=(',',':')))
```

    "oracle_code": "import sys as system, json as codec\nfrom fractions import Fraction as Rational\nfrom pathlib import Path as File\nrecords = codec.loads(File(system.argv[1]).read_text())\nanswer = []\nfor size, past, future, newletter, direction in records:\n    radix = 2 * size + 1\n    a, b = list(past), [newletter] + list(future[1:])\n    if direction > 0:\n        a.insert(0, b.pop(0))\n    if direction < 0:\n        b.insert(0, a.pop(0))\n    values = []\n    for word in (a,b):\n        # Direct positional series plus its exact geometric blank tail.\n        value = sum((Rational(letter,radix**position) for position,letter in enumerate(word,1)), Rational(0))\n        value += Rational(1, (radix-1)*radix**len(word))\n        values.append(str(value.numerator)+'/'+str(value.denominator))\n    answer.append(values)\ncanonical = codec.dumps(answer,separators=(',',':'))\nif system.argv[3] != canonical:\n    system.exit(4)\ntry:\n    observed = codec.loads(File(system.argv[2]).read_text())\nexcept Exception:\n    system.exit(5)\n# Re-check every exact pair; no tolerance is admitted.\nif observed != answer:\n    system.exit(6)\nsystem.exit(0)\n",
    "output_contract": "Exact rational coordinate pairs for the normalized affine tape instruction.",
    "reason": "The normalized rectangle instructions are finite explicit affine maps. The code checks their local tape action; the smooth flow realization and all-time halting equivalence remain outside this finite test.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON list of cases [m, left_digits, right_digits, written_digit, displacement].\n# PRECONDITIONS: 1 <= m <= 3; digit lists have length 1 or 2 and odd digits in [1, 2*m-1]; displacement in {-1,0,1}; infinite tails repeat digit 1.\n# OUTPUTS: JSON pairs of exact rational coordinate strings for the updated tape.\n# TIME: O(cases * digit-list length) rational arithmetic operations; cases <= 2000.\n# SPACE: O(cases * digit-list length) stored exact rational data.\n# ARITHMETIC: python-flint fmpq; no floating point or solver witnesses.\n# FAILURE: Invalid input raises an error; no result outside the finite input contract.\nimport sys, json\nfrom flint import fmpq\ncases = json.loads(sys.stdin.read())\nassert isinstance(cases, list) and 0 < len(cases) <= 2000\ndef coordinate(digits, base):\n    total = fmpq(1, base-1)\n    for digit in reversed(digits):\n        total = (digit + total) / base\n    return total\ndef text(q):\n    return str(q.numerator) + '/' + str(q.denominator)\nresults = []\nfor m, left, right, written, move in cases:\n    assert type(m) is int and 1 <= m <= 3\n    base = 2*m+1\n    assert type(move) is int and move in (-1,0,1)\n    assert all(isinstance(ds,list) and 1 <= len(ds) <= 2 for ds in (left,right))\n    assert all(type(v) is int and v in range(1,2*m,2) for v in left+right+[written])\n    x = coordinate(left, base)\n    y = coordinate(right, base)\n    correction = fmpq(written-right[0], base)\n    if move == 1:\n        nx = (written+x)/base\n        ny = base*y-right[0]\n    elif move == -1:\n        nx = base*x-left[0]\n        ny = (left[0]+y+correction)/base\n    else:\n        nx = x\n        ny = y+correction\n    results.append([text(nx),text(ny)])\nprint(json.dumps(results,separators=(',',':')))\n",

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The finite force evaluator supports a conditional software representation of the prescribed computation; its exact reachability obstruction applies only to the constructed descriptions and observations. |
| games_simulation | True | weak | inspirational | inferred | An exact simulation model can represent the prescribed smooth processor and its detector, but numerical discretization and perturbation tolerance are not supplied. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: the finite force description does not establish a general-purpose engineering tool contract. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The finite local compiler retains instruction history and makes each ordinary transition a finite sequence suitable for adapting a symbolic processor. |
| data_structures | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | Work letters, history letters and marker bits provide a symbolic representation for reversible instruction recording; a bounded-storage implementation is not established. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: the paper establishes prescribed exact fluid computation, but does not supply hardware realization, device arithmetic and accelerator performance. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: the paper establishes prescribed exact fluid computation, but does not supply cryptographic primitives, adversarial guarantees and security parameters. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: the paper establishes prescribed exact fluid computation, but does not supply learning objectives, training procedures and statistical guarantees. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: the paper establishes prescribed exact fluid computation, but does not supply an optimisation objective, a solver contract and an optimality guarantee. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: the paper establishes prescribed exact fluid computation, but does not supply distributed execution, communication protocols and fault models. |
| other (Forced incompressible-flow computability) | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | Forced incompressible-flow computability is directly supported by the effective construction and its fixed material detector. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L1** (hypothesis): Effective evaluation requires a fixed computable positive viscosity; evaluation for a fixed positive real viscosity is relative to that parameter. Blocked use: ['Unqualified computability for an unspecified real viscosity.']
- **L2** (formal_scope): Uniqueness is restricted to the stated comparison classes, and the exhibited solutions do not establish general large-data existence. Blocked use: ['Applying the residual realization argument as a general existence theorem.']
- **L3** (hypothesis): The whole-space regularity and finite-interval bounds constrain competitors as well as the prescribed solution; competitors need not share its compact support. Blocked use: ['Dropping the competitor regularity assumptions in a uniqueness claim.']
- **L4** (hypothesis): A slowdown preserves all-time reachability only when every finite logical time has a finite physical preimage. Blocked use: ['Using a clock with finite accumulated logical time to preserve arbitrary computations.']
- **L5** (approximation): Exact continuum detectors do not supply a uniform perturbation tolerance for the force program, field or observed coordinates. Blocked use: ['Treating finite-precision observation or noisy implementation as an equivalent detector.']
- **L6** (counterexample_scope): The undecidability consequence concerns force descriptions produced by the effective constructions and their fixed observations. Blocked use: ['Transferring this consequence to arbitrary flow descriptions or unrelated verification tasks.']
- **L7** (formal_scope): The local recorders use exact-real tape coordinates without a finite-storage bound and do not establish thermodynamic or computational-complexity conclusions. Blocked use: ['Claiming finite-memory physical computation or complexity and thermodynamic bounds from the encoding.']
- **L8** (hypothesis): A prescribed residual force alone does not specify a finite computational mechanism; local branches, effectivity and complete trajectories require separate construction. Blocked use: ['Inferring a computational processor merely by writing down the residual force identity.']
- **L9** (formal_scope): The planar prefix interpolation need not preserve area; incompressibility belongs to the transverse lift. Blocked use: ['Treating the planar prefix field itself as incompressible.']
- **L10** (edge_case): Initially terminal configurations count as halting, and transitions into terminal states complete their pending move before reaching the terminal checkpoint. Blocked use: ['Stopping before a pending move has completed or excluding initial halting.']
- **L11** (formal_scope): Root-clock bounds apply separately at each fixed mixed derivative order and do not provide a common constant over all orders. Blocked use: ['Using the displayed estimate as an order-independent derivative bound.']
- **L12** (counterexample_scope): An onto slowdown does not supply faster-than-every-power decay for an arbitrary repeated processor. Blocked use: ['Combining an integrable speed with unrestricted all-time computation.']
- **L13** (formal_scope): Periodic and eventually stationary forcing are separate constructions; the stationary construction may have nonzero mean force during startup. Blocked use: ['Assigning mean-zero forcing throughout startup to the stationary construction.']
- **L14** (formal_scope): Making the prefix force divergence free by projection changes the normalized pressure while preserving the velocity. Blocked use: ['Keeping pressure zero by assumption after projecting the residual force.']
- **L15** (hypothesis): The Eulerian detector invokes a companion diffusion theorem and its geometric and variation hypotheses; it is not a standalone consequence of the residual identity. Blocked use: ['Claiming the velocity threshold without the diffusion theorem and its hypotheses.']
- **L16** (formal_scope): The velocity-threshold force has no asserted uniform large-time amplitude bound or mean-zero condition. Blocked use: ['Transferring compact-support or logarithmic-decay force bounds to the velocity-threshold construction.']
- **L17** (formal_scope): The diffusion injections test fresh prefixes from the initial planar point and do not reset the material trajectory. Blocked use: ['Treating each injected scalar bump as a restart of the marked particle.']

{
  "canonical_sha256": "bfaec932281d2527fc3dfdec417ee7fc5cb92e8373699ce0b642da9ba1625edc",
  "engineering_tasks": [
    {
      "basis": "stated",
      "contract_anchor": "stmt-bcs-scalar-theorem",
      "eng_class": "PHYS-SIM",
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-bcs-scalar-theorem",
          "quote": "Fix a positive computable viscosity $\\nu$. A deterministic one-tape machine and finite input effectively determine a smooth force on $[0,\\infty)\\times\\mathbb R^3$, supported in one compact spatial set for all time, whose solution from zero velocity has pressure zero and satisfies $$\\|\\partial_t^jD_x^\\alpha u(t)\\|_\\infty+\n \\|\\partial_t^jD_x^\\alpha f(t)\\|_\\infty\n \\le C_{j,\\alpha}(1+t)^{-1-j}$$ for every $j\\ge0$ and spatial multi-index $\\alpha$. The particle initially at the origin enters $\\{x_1<-1\\}$ exactly when the machine halts. Every displayed derivative belongs to space-time $L^2$, and the kinetic energy is uniformly bounded. Uniqueness holds among smooth classical solutions with, for every finite $T$, $$u\\in C([0,T];H^2)\\cap C^1([0,T];L^2),\\quad\n p\\in C([0,T];H^1),\\quad\n \\sup_{[0,T]\\times\\mathbb R^3}(|u|+|\\nabla u|)<\\infty.$$ The formulas remain valid for any fixed positive real viscosity, with evaluation relative to that parameter.",
          "quote_sha256": "093d89de4b0d99791dbec7ed67703fef823ba7c45ea9ad43f7c4cf78bda5be13"
        }
      ],
      "hypotheses": "Exact real prescriptions in the stated whole-space setting.",
      "input": "Finite machine, finite word and computable viscosity.",
      "maturity": "adaptable",
      "output": "Smooth residual force evaluator and its derivative evaluators.",
      "task": "Compile a finite machine into a compactly supported force evaluator",
      "verification": "PENDING"
    },
    {
      "basis": "stated",
      "contract_anchor": "stmt-bcs-clock",
      "eng_class": "PHYS-SIM",
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-bcs-clock",
          "quote": "Fix $\\nu>0$. Let $W(s,x)$ be a smooth divergence-free field for $s\\ge0$, on a flat torus or on $\\mathbb R^3$. Suppose all its mixed derivatives are bounded, $W(0)=0$, and in the whole-space case its spatial support lies in a fixed compact set. Let $g:[0,\\infty)\\to[0,\\infty)$ be smooth, nondecreasing, onto, with $g(0)=0$. Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n u(t,x)&=g'(t)W(g(t),x),\\\\\n f(t,x)&=g''(t)W(g(t),x)-\\nu g'(t)\\Delta W(g(t),x)\\notag\\\\\n &\\quad +(g'(t))^2[\\partial_sW+(W\\cdot\\nabla)W](g(t),x)\n \\label{bcs:clock-force}\n\\end{aligned}$$ gives a prescribed solution with pressure zero and zero initial velocity. Its material flow is $X_u(t,a)=X_W(g(t),a)$, and hence preserves every all-time reachability event. If $(W\\cdot\\nabla)W=0$ identically, $f$ is divergence free. <a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We construct smooth forces of fixed compact spatial support for three-dimensional incompressible Navier–Stokes flow from rest whose particle at the origin detects halting by entering a fixed half-space. Every mixed derivative of the force and velocity decays at rate $`O((1+t)^{-1-j})`$ for time order <i>j</i>. Three scalar potentials lift a coded rectangle, perform its instruction, and lower its image; an unbounded logarithmic clock supplies the decay. We also realize area-changing prefix instructions on an invariant torus plane, and construct a planar Hamiltonian processor admitting periodic forcing, stationary forcing after startup, and a velocity-field detector through a companion diffusion theorem.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Finite instructions in a viscous flow

A smooth fluid motion is invertible on every finite time interval. A machine instruction may overwrite its scanned symbol and lose the information needed to undo that instruction. To make a fluid particle execute an arbitrary machine, one must resolve this difference and then keep track of what happens between successive encoded configurations. This paper gives explicit geometric realizations with fixed observation sets and controlled forcing.

We work on $\mathbb R^3$ and on the unit flat torus $\mathbb T^3=(\mathbb R/\mathbb Z)^3$. For a fixed viscosity $\nu>0$ the equation is <a id="eq:NS"></a>$$\label{eq:NS}
 \partial_tu+(u\cdot\nabla)u-\nu\Delta u+\nabla p=f,
 \qquad \operatorname{div}u=0,\qquad u(0,\cdot)=0.$$ Our principal compact-support result is the following.

<a id="stmt-bcs-scalar-theorem"></a>
<div id="bcs:scalar-theorem" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *Fix a positive computable viscosity $\nu$. A deterministic one-tape machine and finite input effectively determine a smooth force on $[0,\infty)\times\mathbb R^3$, supported in one compact spatial set for all time, whose solution from zero velocity has pressure zero and satisfies $$\|\partial_t^jD_x^\alpha u(t)\|_\infty+
 \|\partial_t^jD_x^\alpha f(t)\|_\infty
 \le C_{j,\alpha}(1+t)^{-1-j}$$ for every $j\ge0$ and spatial multi-index $\alpha$. The particle initially at the origin enters $\{x_1<-1\}$ exactly when the machine halts. Every displayed derivative belongs to space-time $L^2$, and the kinetic energy is uniformly bounded. Uniqueness holds among smooth classical solutions with, for every finite $T$, $$u\in C([0,T];H^2)\cap C^1([0,T];L^2),\quad
 p\in C([0,T];H^1),\quad
 \sup_{[0,T]\times\mathbb R^3}(|u|+|\nabla u|)<\infty.$$ The formulas remain valid for any fixed positive real viscosity, with evaluation relative to that parameter.*

</div>

The input is a finite deterministic one-tape machine and a finite word. The output is a finite program for evaluating the force $f(t,x)$ and each requested mixed derivative to arbitrary rational accuracy. The program specifies every local instruction branch. It does not determine the machine’s later configurations and then prescribe their replay. Numerical effectivity uses a fixed computable viscosity; the same formulas can also be evaluated relative to any fixed real $\nu>0$.

For a material label $a$, write $X_u(t,a)$ for the solution of $\partial_tX_u(t,a)=u(t,X_u(t,a))$, $X_u(0,a)=a$. A material detector asks whether this one particle ever enters a fixed open set $O$. The label and set are independent of the machine and input. The forces constructed here have unique global smooth solutions in the precise comparison classes below, so the detector has an unambiguous meaning.

The geometric question behind this theorem is: how can one instruction’s image overlap another instruction’s source without confusing their motions? We give each source rectangle a separate height. The lift selects the source, the planar phase performs its positive reciprocal affine map, and the descent selects the image. Separate disjointness of the source family and image family is sufficient. A delayed-head recorder makes that image condition hold, and every tracked path has a first coordinate between its endpoints. This last property proves the detector statement at all real times.

Two further questions lead to different constructions. Prefix replacements can change planar area. Section <a href="#bcs:prefix-section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bcs:prefix-section">6</a> realizes them by a planar motion with transverse divergence compensation: the plane containing the computation stays invariant while nearby transverse volume changes compensate planar compression. The resulting torus force has the same logarithmic derivative decay, and the fixed particle $(1/4,1/2,1/2)$ detects halting in the right half of the first coordinate.

For a spatial suspension, the third coordinate must instead remain available as a clock. Section <a href="#bcs:expanded-section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bcs:expanded-section">7</a> therefore routes full rectangles within the plane itself. Its Hamiltonian field gives periodic forcing from rest and, with a distinct startup profile, forcing that is stationary after time one. The latter has a nonzero vertical mean during startup, computed explicitly in Proposition <a href="#bcs:expanded-material" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bcs:expanded-material">12</a>. Effective forward and inverse derivative bounds also let us apply the injection, stirring and waiting theorem of the companion (OpenAI 2026, Theorem 3.1) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[velocity2026](#ref-velocity2026)]. The resulting observation tests the third velocity component on a fixed strip, rather than following a marked particle.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### History of the ideas and the role of the forcing

Turing’s undecidable symbol-printing question supplies the logical obstruction behind the final reachability corollary (Turing 1936--1937, sec. 8) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Turing1936](#ref-Turing1936)]. A machine can be made to halt when it prints the specified symbol, while each ordinary stop without that printing is redirected into an infinite dummy loop. This connects that formulation to the halting convention used here. Landauer discussed retaining intermediate information to avoid logical irreversibility (Landauer 1961, sec. 3) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Landauer1961](#ref-Landauer1961)]; Bennett implemented reversible computation by recording a history of transitions (Bennett 1973) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Bennett1973](#ref-Bennett1973)]. Our explicit local recorders use that information-retention principle. Their exact-real tape coordinates have no finite-storage bound, and we do not import thermodynamic or computational-complexity conclusions.

Moore’s generalized shifts encode tapes by positional expansions and turn local symbolic rules into piecewise affine maps (Moore 1990, 1991) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Moore1990](#ref-Moore1990), [Moore1991](#ref-Moore1991)]. His smooth realizations and suspensions explain the symbolic-to-dynamical framework used here. Cardona, Miranda, Peralta-Salas and Presas realize bijective generalized shifts by area-preserving disk diffeomorphisms and suspend them in a geometric construction of stationary Euler flows (Cardona et al. 2021, Proposition 5.1 and Theorem 3.1) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[CardonaMirandaPeraltaSalasPresas2021](#ref-CardonaMirandaPeraltaSalasPresas2021)]. Their Euler realization uses an adapted Riemannian metric. Cardona, Miranda and Peralta-Salas later constructed a Turing-complete stationary Euler field for the Euclidean metric on $\mathbb R^3$; its computational invariant set is noncompact and the field has infinite energy (Cardona, Miranda, and Peralta-Salas 2023, arXiv v3, Theorem 1 and the following discussion) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[CardonaMirandaPeraltaSalas2023](#ref-CardonaMirandaPeraltaSalas2023)]. We work in fixed flat coordinates and explicitly prescribe a viscous force from zero initial velocity. The local proofs below supply their own closed-rectangle action, support, quantitative bounds and intermediate observation paths.

Dyhr, González-Prieto, Miranda and Peralta-Salas construct unforced stationary Turing-complete Navier–Stokes fields with Hodge viscosity on compact three-manifolds admitting a nowhere-zero harmonic one-form, after a generally non-small metric deformation (Dyhr et al. 2026, Theorem A) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[DyhrGonzalezPrietoMirandaPeraltaSalas2026](#ref-DyhrGonzalezPrietoMirandaPeraltaSalas2026)]. That geometric result supplies viscous context; the fixed-flat-metric, zero-initial-data force prescriptions here are proved separately.

Cardona, Miranda and Peralta-Salas also realize computation through the unforced Euler evolution on a constructed high-dimensional compact Riemannian manifold with a specially chosen metric: the computational input is encoded in the initial velocity, and the observation is entry into an open set of smooth divergence-free velocity fields (Cardona, Miranda, and Peralta-Salas 2022, arXiv v1, Theorem 1.1, Remark 1.2 and Definition 5.1) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[CardonaMirandaPeraltaSalas2022](#ref-CardonaMirandaPeraltaSalas2022)]. This supplies a velocity-field antecedent to the observation used in our diffusion application.

For a prescribed divergence-free velocity $U$, the identity $f=U_t+(U\cdot\nabla)U-\nu\Delta U$ always makes $(U,0)$ a solution. It does not by itself construct a finite computational mechanism. The work lies in defining $U$ from all local instruction branches, proving its effectivity, and controlling the complete trajectories and force derivatives. The analytic uniqueness step is the classical energy comparison associated with Leray (Leray 1934, sec. 18) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[Leray1934](#ref-Leray1934)], proved locally in Section <a href="#sec:analytic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:analytic">2</a> for the classes we use.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Proof organization

Section <a href="#sec:analytic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:analytic">2</a> fixes the analytic comparison classes and proves the residual realization lemma. Section <a href="#bcs:clocks-section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bcs:clocks-section">3</a> proves the onto-clock identity and logarithmic estimate, emphasizing that accumulated computational time must remain unbounded. Section <a href="#bcs:scalar-section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bcs:scalar-section">4</a> proves the compact-support theorem by a complete compiler, coding and scalar-potential argument. Section <a href="#bcs:clock-options" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bcs:clock-options">5</a> then gives the optional root clocks and spatial startup profiles. Section <a href="#bcs:prefix-section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bcs:prefix-section">6</a> replaces reciprocal planar maps by general prefix maps and compensates their area change. Section <a href="#bcs:expanded-section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bcs:expanded-section">7</a> keeps the realization planar and derives the suspension and diffusion applications. Section <a href="#sec:decision" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:decision">8</a> states the decision consequence and the scope of the exact detector.

<a id="sec:analytic"></a>
### Prescribed solutions and their uniqueness

<a id="eq:comparison"></a>
A construction below first specifies a smooth velocity $U$ and then defines its residual force. We need uniqueness only for those data. On a torus, the comparison class consists of classical velocities $v$ with $v,v_t$ and spatial derivatives through order two continuous on every finite closed time cylinder, and periodic pressures $p$ with $p,\nabla p$ continuous and mean zero. On $\mathbb R^3$, we use smooth classical solutions satisfying, for each $T<\infty$, $$\label{eq:comparison}
 v\in C([0,T];H^2)\cap C^1([0,T];L^2),\qquad
 p\in C([0,T];H^1),\qquad
 \sup_{[0,T]\times\mathbb R^3}(|v|+|\nabla v|)<\infty.$$ These conditions concern competitors as well as the exhibited solution. In the whole-space constructions the reference velocity has fixed compact support; a competitor is not required to have that support.

<a id="stmt-lem-b-comparison"></a>
<div id="lem:b-comparison" class="lemma">

**Lemma 2** (Residual realization). *Fix $\nu>0$ and let $U$ be a prescribed smooth divergence-free velocity with $U(0)=0$, bounded together with its spatial gradient on every finite time interval. In the whole-space case assume also the velocity conditions in <a href="#eq:comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:comparison">[eq:comparison]</a>. Then the force $$f=\mathcal F_\nu[U]:=U_t+(U\cdot\nabla)U-\nu\Delta U$$ has $(U,0)$ as its unique solution in the stated comparison class. Its material trajectories exist uniquely for all finite forward times and give smooth diffeomorphisms at each such time. A mean-zero velocity on the torus has a mean-zero residual force.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-b-comparison"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-b-comparison</summary>

*Proof.* Substitution proves existence. If $(v,p)$ is another solution, put $w=v-U$. The difference equation is $$w_t+(v\cdot\nabla)w+(w\cdot\nabla)U
       =\nu\Delta w-\nabla p,\qquad w(0)=0,\quad\operatorname{div}w=0.$$ On the torus, multiply by $w$ and integrate periodically. Transport by $v$ and pressure give zero, and diffusion gives $-\nu\|\nabla w\|_2^2$. The stated classical regularity justifies these integrations and time differentiation.

On $\mathbb R^3$, insert a smooth cutoff $\chi_R$ equal to one on the ball of radius $R$, supported in the ball of radius $2R$, and with $|\nabla\chi_R|\le C/R$. The boundary errors in the transport, pressure and diffusion integrations are bounded respectively by $$CR^{-1}\|v\|_\infty\|w\|_2^2,\qquad
 CR^{-1}\|p\|_2\|w\|_2,\qquad
 CR^{-1}\|\nabla w\|_2\|w\|_2.$$ They tend to zero. The $C^1(L^2)$ condition justifies differentiation of the squared norm; $C(H^2)$ and the finite-interval bounds justify the limiting products and integrations. Equivalently one can pass to the integrated-in-time identity, with the same estimates uniform on each finite interval. In either domain the result is $$\frac12\frac{d}{dt}\|w\|_2^2+\nu\|\nabla w\|_2^2
 \le\|\nabla U\|_{\infty,\mathrm{op}}\|w\|_2^2.$$ Gronwall’s inequality gives $w=0$. The equation then gives $\nabla p=0$. Mean-zero normalization on the torus fixes $p=0$; on $\mathbb R^3$, the only spatially constant $H^1$ pressure is zero.

Bounded speed prevents finite-time escape of a material trajectory. Local spatial Lipschitz continuity gives uniqueness, and solving backwards on a finite interval gives the inverse flow. Smooth dependence on the initial point gives its smoothness. Finally, the torus integral of $\Delta U$ and of $(U\cdot\nabla)U=\operatorname{div}(U\otimes U)$ vanishes, proving the mean assertion. ◻

</details>

The lemma is the classical difference-energy argument; it is not a general large-data existence theorem. All forces below come with their explicitly constructed global smooth solution. A smooth compactly supported velocity with the derivative bounds we establish automatically belongs to the whole-space velocity class in <a href="#eq:comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:comparison">[eq:comparison]</a>.

<a id="bcs:clocks-section"></a>
### Changing the speed without losing computation

A slower flow executes the same instructions only if its new clock reaches every finite logical time. The unforced viscous continuation in (Cardona et al. 2021, sec. 6.B) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[CardonaMirandaPeraltaSalasPresas2021](#ref-CardonaMirandaPeraltaSalasPresas2021)] uses a speed $Me^{-\nu t}$, with $M>0$, and has only the finite total logical time $M/\nu$. Here a prescribed residual force allows the nonintegrable speed $(1+t)^{-1}$. The logarithmic estimate below is the only clock bound needed for the first compact construction. Other onto clocks and spatial startup profiles follow that complete application in Section <a href="#bcs:clock-options" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bcs:clock-options">5</a>. The force is always defined from the prescribed field; it is not inferred from an unknown solution.

<a id="bcs:smooth-step"></a>
We use the explicit smooth step $$\label{bcs:smooth-step}
 \rho(v)=\begin{cases}e^{-1/v},&v>0,\\0,&v\le0,\end{cases}
 \qquad \sigma(v)=\frac{\rho(v)}{\rho(v)+\rho(1-v)}.$$ It is zero for $v\le0$, one for $v\ge1$, and satisfies $\sigma(v)+\sigma(1-v)=1$. Each positive-side derivative of $\rho$ is $e^{-1/v}$ times a polynomial in $1/v$ and extends flatly by zero. The bounds $y^m e^{-y}\le(m+k)!y^{-k}$ give effective errors near the joining point, while the denominator of $\sigma$ is at least $e^{-2}$. Thus all these functions and derivatives can be evaluated without an exact equality test at a seam. For phase intervals we use $\zeta(s)=\sigma(2s-1/2)$, which is constant near both endpoints.

<a id="stmt-bcs-clock"></a>
<div id="bcs:clock" class="lemma">

<a id="bcs:clock-force"></a>
**Lemma 3** (Onto changes of clock). *Fix $\nu>0$. Let $W(s,x)$ be a smooth divergence-free field for $s\ge0$, on a flat torus or on $\mathbb R^3$. Suppose all its mixed derivatives are bounded, $W(0)=0$, and in the whole-space case its spatial support lies in a fixed compact set. Let $g:[0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)$ be smooth, nondecreasing, onto, with $g(0)=0$. Then $$\begin{aligned}
 u(t,x)&=g'(t)W(g(t),x),\\
 f(t,x)&=g''(t)W(g(t),x)-\nu g'(t)\Delta W(g(t),x)\notag\\
 &\quad +(g'(t))^2[\partial_sW+(W\cdot\nabla)W](g(t),x)
 \label{bcs:clock-force}
\end{aligned}$$ gives a prescribed solution with pressure zero and zero initial velocity. Its material flow is $X_u(t,a)=X_W(g(t),a)$, and hence preserves every all-time reachability event. If $(W\cdot\nabla)W=0$ identically, $f$ is divergence free. Spatial mean zero, when imposed on $W$, is inherited by $u$ and $f$.*

<a id="bcs:log-bounds"></a>
*For $g(t)=\log(1+t)$, every $j\ge0$ and spatial multi-index $\alpha$ satisfy $$\label{bcs:log-bounds}
 \|\partial_t^jD_x^\alpha u(t)\|_\infty+
 \|\partial_t^jD_x^\alpha f(t)\|_\infty
 \le C_{j,\alpha}(1+t)^{-1-j}.$$ All these derivatives belong to $L^2([0,\infty);L^\infty)$; on a torus or with fixed compact support they also belong to space-time $L^2$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-bcs-clock"><summary>Proof of stmt-bcs-clock</summary>

*Proof.* The chain rule gives <a href="#bcs:clock-force" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bcs:clock-force">[bcs:clock-force]</a> as $u_t+(u\cdot\nabla)u-\nu\Delta u$. The divergence statements follow by differentiation; the convection term is absent under the additional shear hypothesis. On a torus its spatial integral is zero because $(W\cdot\nabla)W=\operatorname{div}(W\otimes W)$. The same chain rule applied to $X_W(g(t),a)$ proves the trajectory identity. Onto means that every finite logical time has a finite preimage. This proves both directions of the event equivalence, including a visit during initialization. Existence is supplied by the explicit field, and uniqueness in the appropriate comparison class is Lemma <a href="#lem:b-comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:b-comparison">2</a>.

For the logarithmic choice put $a=(1+t)^{-1}$ and $s=\log(1+t)$. For any smooth bounded-derivative function $A$ and positive integer $r$, $$\partial_t[a^rA(s,x)]=a^{r+1}(\partial_s-r)A(s,x).$$ The velocity is $aW$, while the force is $$-\nu a\Delta W(s,x)+a^2[\partial_sW-W+(W\cdot\nabla)W](s,x).$$ Repeated use of the displayed identity, commuting spatial derivatives, proves <a href="#bcs:log-bounds" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bcs:log-bounds">[bcs:log-bounds]</a>. Integrating $(1+t)^{-2-2j}$ gives the stated norms. Fixed support also gives uniformly bounded kinetic energy. None of these calculations requires the executed instruction sequence. ◻

</details>

<a id="bcs:scalar-section"></a>
### A compact realization by three scalar potentials

A planar instruction may be easy to interpolate even when its image overlaps another instruction’s source. A third coordinate resolves this overlap: lift each source rectangle to its own height, perform its planar map there, and lower it using its image rectangle. This section makes that procedure into a complete machine-to-fluid construction. Zero is the blank digit, so some encoded points lie on interval endpoints; every cutoff below is one on a neighborhood of the full closed rectangle.

We now prove Theorem <a href="#bcs:scalar-theorem" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bcs:scalar-theorem">1</a>. The proof first makes the finite instruction table injective, then realizes its full rectangles, and finally changes the physical clock.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### Recording an instruction before moving the head

We first produce a finite local table whose images can be distinguished. Retaining erased information is the history method of reversible computation (Bennett 1973) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Bennett1973](#ref-Bennett1973)]. Here the ordinary head displacement is delayed until the history has been written and the recorder has returned.

Let $A$ be the work alphabet, $Q$ the state set, and $q_0$ the initial state. Identify all designated halt states with one state $h$, redirect transitions into them to $h$, and replace an initially terminal state by $h$. Replace each missing instruction at $(q,a)$ by the stationary symbol-preserving instruction $(q,a)\mapsto(h,a,0)$. A transition into $h$ completes its prescribed write and move before halting. For a one-sided tape, include an immutable end-marker track at cell zero in $A$ and retain its prescribed boundary action in the finite table; every write preserves that track. Write $\delta(q,a)=(q^+,b,d^+)$ with $d^+\in\{-1,0,1\}$. Let $$I=\{(q,d,a):q\ne h,\ d\in\{-1,0,1\},\ a\in A\},\qquad
 \mathcal H=\{\bot,P\}\sqcup\{[i]:i\in I\}.$$ A tape cell has a work letter, a history letter in $\mathcal H$, and a bit. The auxiliary states are $R_{q,d},B_{q,d},L_{q,d}$ and $F_i$. At a ready state $R_{q,d}$ after $n$ ordinary steps, the intended invariant has its frontier at cell $2+n$, at least two cells to the right of the work head; records occupy cells $2,\ldots,1+n$, all remaining history cells except the frontier are blank, and all bits are zero. The recorder writes the work letter and marks the old head, scans right to append a history record, and returns to erase that mark. Only then does it make the ordinary head displacement, restoring a ready state; the proof below verifies the invariant and finite scan lengths.

The following rules, expanded over the indicated letters, are the entire table.

1.  In $R_{q,d_0}$ with $q\ne h$, on $(a,\gamma,0)$ with $\gamma\ne P$, write $(b,\gamma,1)$, move right, and enter $F_i$, where $i=(q,d_0,a)$.

2.  In $F_i$, move right over unmarked cells with history different from $P$. On $(v,P,0)$, write $(v,[i],0)$, move right, and enter $B_{q^+,d^+}$.

3.  In $B_{q,d}$, on $(v,\bot,0)$, write $(v,P,0)$, move left, and enter $L_{q,d}$.

4.  In $L_{q,d}$, move left over unmarked cells whose history is not $P$. On $(v,\gamma,1)$ with $\gamma\ne P$, erase the bit, move by $d$, and enter $R_{q,d}$.

Initialize in $R_{q_0,0}$ with head at zero and the given finite work input, blank work elsewhere. The history track has $P$ at cell $2$ and $\bot$ at every other cell; every marker bit is zero.

<a id="stmt-bcs-delayed-recorder"></a>
<div id="bcs:delayed-recorder" class="lemma">

**Lemma 4**. *At successive ready states the auxiliary table has exactly the ordinary work tape, head and state. It reaches an $R_{h,d}$ exactly when the ordinary machine halts. Every ordinary step uses a positive finite number of auxiliary steps. On the full domain of the auxiliary table, a destination state fixes the incoming displacement, and that state together with the full written symbol determines the incoming rule.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-bcs-delayed-recorder"><summary>Proof of stmt-bcs-delayed-recorder</summary>

*Proof.* After $n$ completed ordinary steps, put $k=2+n$. The unique frontier $P$ is at $k$, cells $2,\ldots,k-1$ contain the records, and all other history cells are blank. At a ready state all bits are zero and the head $j$ satisfies $k-j\ge2$. The first rule changes the work letter and marks $j$. The right scan reaches the frontier, writes the record, creates the frontier at $k+1$, and returns to the sole marked cell. Erasing that mark and then moving by $d^+$ restores the invariant, since $(k+1)-(j+d^+)\ge2$. Both scans cross finite intervals. An initial halt requires no ordinary transition.

For the inverse assertion, into $F_i$ the index $i$ identifies the original ready state and read work letter, while the written bit distinguishes entry from the scanning loop. An arrival at $B_{q,d}$ writes a record $[i]$, which identifies the forward state; its work letter is retained. Into $L_{q,d}$, the frontier written on entry distinguishes that rule from the backward loop. An arrival at $R_{q,d}$ is the marker-erasure rule with displacement $d$, and the written work and history letters recover the read symbol. Within each family the retained letters distinguish the remaining choices. These checks use all allowed symbols and do not assume initialization. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### From the local table to full rectangles

The positional encoding follows the generalized-shift viewpoint of Moore (Moore 1990, 1991) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[Moore1990](#ref-Moore1990), [Moore1991](#ref-Moore1991)]; its explicit gap and endpoint estimates are proved here. Order the alphabet $\Gamma=A\times\mathcal H\times\{0,1\}$ with its all-blank symbol first. Give its letters the digits $c(a)=2\operatorname{index}(a)$, starting at index zero, in base $G=2|\Gamma|$. Put $$E(a_1a_2\cdots)=\sum_{n\ge1}c(a_n)G^{-n},\qquad
 I_a=[c(a)/G,(c(a)+1)/G].$$ The digits range from $0$ to $G-2$. Distinct first-letter intervals have a gap at least $G^{-1}$, and $GE(a\omega)-c(a)=E(\omega)$ uniquely decodes the tails, including the zero blank tail. Give the states distinct positive indices. Set $o_s$ equal to three times its index for nonterminal states, and minus three times its index for $R_{h,d}$. A relative tape with head at $j$ is encoded by $$(x,y)=\bigl(o_s+E(w_{j-1}w_{j-2}\cdots),E(w_jw_{j+1}\cdots)\bigr).$$ At an initialized ready state the consecutive records immediately left of the frontier give their number $n$, so the frontier has absolute index $2+n$. Its relative distance from the head recovers the absolute head index as well.

Split each local rule $(s,a)\mapsto(s',b,d)$ by the letter $l$ immediately to the left, and index the resulting geometric branches by $i$. This index labels the split branches, rather than the recorder triples used above. The source of branch $i$ is $C_i=(o_s+I_l)\times I_a$, and the affine instruction is <a id="bcs:scalar-branches"></a>$$\label{bcs:scalar-branches}
 \Theta_i(x,y)=\begin{cases}
 (o_{s'}+(c(b)+x-o_s)/G,\ Gy-c(a)),&d=1,\\
 (o_{s'}+G(x-o_s)-c(l),\ (c(l)+y+(c(b)-c(a))/G)/G),&d=-1,\\
 (o_{s'}+x-o_s,\ y+(c(b)-c(a))/G),&d=0.
 \end{cases}$$ Removing and prefixing digits proves the update on every applicable code. Its linear part is $\operatorname{diag}(\lambda_i,\lambda_i^{-1})$ with positive $\lambda_i$. Its full image rectangle is, respectively, $$(o_{s'}+(c(b)+I_l)/G)\times[0,1],\quad
 (o_{s'}+[0,1])\times(c(l)+I_b)/G,\quad
 (o_{s'}+I_l)\times I_b.$$ The source family is separated. The image family is separated too: within a destination the displacement is fixed by Lemma <a href="#bcs:delayed-recorder" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bcs:delayed-recorder">4</a>, and the pair $(l,b)$ identifies the split rule. Distinct such pairs have distinct two-digit intervals in a moving case, or separated coordinate products in the stationary case. Every gap is at least $G^{-2}$. This verifies the needed geometric property on full rectangles, not just on encoded points.

<a id="pm-h-10"></a>
#### Lifting, interpolation, and lowering

For a closed interval $[a,b]$ and $\varepsilon>0$ use $$\eta_{[a,b]}^\varepsilon(v)=
 \sigma(2(v-a+\varepsilon)/\varepsilon)
 \sigma(2(b+\varepsilon-v)/\varepsilon),$$ where $\sigma$ is the smooth step in <a href="#bcs:smooth-step" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="bcs:smooth-step">[bcs:smooth-step]</a>. It is one on a neighborhood of the closed interval and vanishes outside its $\varepsilon$ enlargement. Products give rectangle cutoffs. With $\varepsilon=1/(4G^2)$, choose $g_i$ for $C_i$ and $\bar g_i$ for $\Theta_i(C_i)$; within each family their supports are disjoint.

Write $\Theta_i(x,y)=(\lambda_i x+\alpha_i,\lambda_i^{-1}y+\beta_i)$ and let $N$ be the number of branches. Put $h_i=3i$. Choose $\eta_i(z)$ equal to one near $h_i$ and supported within distance one of it. Choose $\vartheta(z)$ equal to one on a neighborhood of $[0,3N]$ and compactly supported. Let $O=1+\max_s|o_s|$, and choose an integer $A$ greater than $O+1$ and every number $$\max(1,\lambda_i)O+|\alpha_i|,\qquad
 \max(1,\lambda_i^{-1})+|\beta_i|.$$ Choose a compactly supported planar cutoff $\Xi$ equal to one near $[-A,A]^2$. Define the two divergence-free differential operators $$D_yP=(-\partial_zP,0,\partial_xP),\qquad
 D_zP=(\partial_yP,-\partial_xP,0).$$ They act on scalar potentials; their divergence vanishes because mixed partial derivatives commute.

Figure <a href="#fig:scalar-stages" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:scalar-stages">1</a> shows why separate source and image families suffice. A source is selected before lifting; its image is selected only after the planar map.

<figure id="fig:scalar-stages">

<figcaption>The three scalar-potential phases for one instruction. This is a schematic projection onto the $x, z$ coordinates; the second planar coordinate is suppressed. Distinct branches use separate heights. Source rectangles are disjoint among themselves, as are image rectangles, while a source may overlap an image. During the middle phase the first coordinate stays between its endpoints.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="pm-figsrc-fig-scalar-stages"></a>
**Figure source and visual witness** (`fig:scalar-stages`)

 node[midway,left] {lift};

 node[midway,above] {reciprocal affine interpolation};
